## AOP channel helper, offline continuation

Android USB/ADB boot_completed1 confirmed before this work. Added an
offline-only `waffle_aop_qmp_channel_roundtrip` in waffle_aop.h. It requires
the caller to hold the shared AOP lock and supply a bounded wait callback.
It accepts only the exact established-link/closed-AP-channel state observed
by AOP45, refuses a pending mailbox payload, opens the AP channel, waits for
AOP channel/ACK, then closes AP channel and checks DOWN ACK and retained
link. Only AP channel and remote channel ACK are written; link and payload
are untouched. Returns distinct refusal, timeout and cleanup failure.
It is NOT wired into any hardware probe; supported stages remain 1..45.

`tools/tests/waffle-aop-host.py` passed ASan/UBSan: successful roundtrip,
each of three wait failures (including cleanup failure), and 13 initial
state/geometry/busy refusals with zero writes. Protocol was compared with
Android common qcom_aoss.c at commit
0fc75219fe9a3c90631453e9870e4f6d956f0ebc and existing port Wi-Fi/ADSP.
No hardware test ran in this continuation. Next integrate MMIO/IPCC
callbacks and shared lock into a new isolated probe, with bounded waits
and explicit result markers, before any ACD command. The existing validated
hardware artifact remains `diagnostic-gpu-aop45-unmappable/` and predates
this offline helper. Previous full checkpoint: aop45-readonly.

## 2026-10-28 AOP mailbox preflight

Phone was Android ADB `1c62e9aa`, `sys.boot_completed=1` before testing.
Native `gen7_gmu_first_boot` sends `{class: gpu, res: acd, val: 1}` to AOP
before CX/GPU startup when ACD is enabled. Existing port Wi-Fi/ADSP use the
same AOSS QMP shared memory at `0x0c300000` and IPCC doorbell; stock PJD110
DT names `qcom,aoss-qmp` at `0x0c300000`, size `0x400`, with GPU mailbox via
`qcom,qmp-aop`. Added isolated stage45 to read QMP header, bounds and link
state only, without GPU startup or any AOP/GMU write. It uses temporary
`ml_io_map_unmappable` with `VM_WIMG_IO` and a page-size mapping, while
validating mailbox geometry against the DT's actual `0x400` extent. Added
host bounds/link test and mapped/unmapped mock scenario; full XNU/EFI and
container validation passed in `diagnostic-gpu-aop45-unmappable/`.

Hardware attempts:

- The first launch never reached the kernel because `run_waffle_ios.py`
  still rejected stage45. Its wrapper returned the phone to Android and
  confirmed ADB boot_completed1.
- `runs/20261028T141735Z` read a valid QMP header but panicked while
  unmapping a `0x400` ordinary `ml_io_map` mapping. Android returned ADB1.
- `runs/20261028T141925Z` read the same header but panicked while trying to
  remove a page-size **permanent** `ml_io_map` mapping. Android returned ADB1.
  XNU's `io_map` implementation sets `KMA_PERMANENT` unless `unmappable=true`.
- `runs/20261028T142212Z` used `ml_io_map_unmappable`, read the header,
  logged `AOP QMP preflight complete`, returned to loader with no kernel
  panic, and automatically returned to Android; ADB boot_completed1.
  Header: magic `4d41494c` (MAIL), version1, our mailbox offset `0x128`,
  size728, valid within `0x400`. Their link/channel and our link were UP;
  our channel and ACK were DOWN, so shared QMP channel was not open. The
  runner wrongly marked this as failure because its old validator demanded
  GMU markers for stage45. This was fixed after the run; the unmodified
  `hardware-result-45.json` exit status is retained, with explicit
  `probe_passed_from_kernel_log=true` and `runner_false_negative` added.
  No new hardware test is needed to correct that classification.

Next: implement a bounded QMP link/channel open using existing Wi-Fi/ADSP
protocol and shared lock, with rollback for only the state this probe owns.
Before sending ACD to AOP, add an explicit off/cleanup path and validate the
packet and ACK semantics. Do not retry BCL44/CP RAW unchanged. After every
hardware test return to Android and confirm boot. No frames or acceleration
have been confirmed. RAM only; no flash/erase.

## 2026-10-28 hardware retry and current stop point

Phone initially appeared as OnePlus 12 over USB; ADB `sys.boot_completed=1`.
Booted the checked DMS41 diagnostic UEFI image from Android fastboot, user
selected Boot iOS, and xnuloader reported platform waffle. Ran RAM-only
probe42 once through `run-publish-test.py` at 2026-10-28T13:48:52Z.
`work/waffle/runs/20261028T134852Z/host.log` confirms payload/system transfer
and accepted `oem run 0`, then `getvar result` failed with `usb_read failed:
No such device`. Host and user observed Qualcomm `05c6:900e`. No kernel log,
CP ACK, frame, or acceleration evidence exists for this attempt. Result JSON
records `hardware_tested=true`, `kernel_probe_started=null`, runner exit 1.
User manually recovered the phone; host then confirmed ADB device 1c62e9aa
and `sys.boot_completed=1`. Do not repeat probe42 unchanged.

Offline comparison with `native-matched-port-first-boot.json` and pinned
`vendor/adreno_hfi.h` found another real HFI difference: port labels feature
ID11 `IFPC_OFF`, but ID11 is BCL. It sends ID11 enable0, while native sends
BCL ID11 enable1 data1. Native also sends ACD ID12 enable1 with table, IFPC
ID9 enable1 hysteresis0x1680, and bailout timer ID17 enable1 before CORE;
port omits these and explicitly sends NAP ID10 enable0. These are candidate
prerequisite differences, not a proven cause of 900e. A future experiment
should isolate one difference with a prepare-only control before any RAW test.
The older Sahara diagnostic capture is high-entropy/opaque and did not decode
a crash reason. No additional hardware test was run after the 900e recovery.

`run-publish-test.py` now exits nonzero when the probe fails or Android is not
ADB-confirmed. It has not been rerun on hardware since that exit-code edit.

The 2026-10-28 user request explicitly resumed this port. The older pause
instructions and results below are historical. RAM-only; no flash/erase.
After every hardware test return to Android and confirm that it booted.
